# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title flights Model

class Airline {
    flights : List<Flight>
    addFlight(f : Flight) : void
    closeFlight(flightId : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    publishFlight(f : Flight, now : EDate) : boolean
    removeFlight(f : Flight) : void
    searchFlights(origin : String, dest : String, date : EDate) : List<Flight>
}
class Airport {
    id : String
    servesForCities : List<City>
    addCity(c : City) : void
}
class Booking {
    customer : Customer
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    createReservation(f : Flight, passenger : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class City {
}
class Customer {
    bookings : List<Booking>
    addBooking(f : Flight, now : EDate, listOfPassengerNames : List<String>) : boolean
    cancel(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    confirm(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Flight {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    id : String
    openForBooking : boolean
    arrivalAirport : Airport
    departureAirport : Airport
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    stopovers : List<Stopover>
    addStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
    getConfirmedReservations() : List<Reservation>
    removeStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Passenger {
    name : String
}
class Reservation {
    id : String
    status : ReservationStatus
    flight : Flight
    passenger : Passenger
}
class Stopover {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    airport : Airport
}
enum ReservationStatus {
    PENDING
    CONFIRMED
    CANCELED
}

Airline *-- "*" Flight : flights
Airport --> "*" City : servesForCities
Booking --> Customer : customer
Booking *-- "*" Reservation : reservations
Customer --> "*" Booking : bookings
Flight --> Airport : arrivalAirport
Flight --> Airport : departureAirport
Flight --> "*" Reservation : reservations
Flight *-- "*" Stopover : stopovers
Reservation --> Flight : flight
Reservation --> Passenger : passenger
Stopover --> Airport : airport
@enduml
```

You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title flights Model

class Airline {
    flights : List<Flight>
    addFlight(f : Flight) : void
    closeFlight(flightId : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    publishFlight(f : Flight, now : EDate) : boolean
    removeFlight(f : Flight) : void
    searchFlights(origin : String, dest : String, date : EDate) : List<Flight>
}
class Airport {
    id : String
    servesForCities : List<City>
    addCity(c : City) : void
}
class Booking {
    customer : Customer
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    createReservation(f : Flight, passenger : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class City {
}
class Customer {
    bookings : List<Booking>
    addBooking(f : Flight, now : EDate, listOfPassengerNames : List<String>) : boolean
    cancel(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    confirm(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Flight {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    id : String
    openForBooking : boolean
    arrivalAirport : Airport
    departureAirport : Airport
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    stopovers : List<Stopover>
    addStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
    getConfirmedReservations() : List<Reservation>
    removeStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Passenger {
    name : String
}
class Reservation {
    id : String
    status : ReservationStatus
    flight : Flight
    passenger : Passenger
}
class Stopover {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    airport : Airport
}
enum ReservationStatus {
    PENDING
    CONFIRMED
    CANCELED
}

Airline *-- "*" Flight : flights
Airport --> "*" City : servesForCities
Booking --> Customer : customer
Booking *-- "*" Reservation : reservations
Customer --> "*" Booking : bookings
Flight --> Airport : arrivalAirport
Flight --> Airport : departureAirport
Flight --> "*" Reservation : reservations
Flight *-- "*" Stopover : stopovers
Reservation --> Flight : flight
Reservation --> Passenger : passenger
Stopover --> Airport : airport
@enduml
```

```plantuml
class Airline {

    closeFlight(flightId : String, now : EDate) : boolean
```

Booking::createReservation
Summary: Generates a pending reservation for a specific passenger on a given flight within a booking context.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: reservations
Related Classes: Reservation, Passenger, ReservationStatus, Flight, String

Output: boolean - true upon successful creation.
Pre-condition: f and passenger are valid. No duplicate passenger already exists for f in this booking (caller responsibility).
Post-condition: A new reservation with status PENDING is added to this.reservations.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

Algorithm: 
1. If f is null or f.openForBooking is false, return false.
2. If now >= f.departureTime, return false.
3. For each r in this.reservations:
     If r.flight equals f AND r.passenger.name equals passenger, return false.
4. Generate a unique reservation ID string.
5. Instantiate a new Passenger object using the provided passenger name.
6. Instantiate a new Reservation object, setting its id to the generated unique ID, status to ReservationStatus.PENDING, flight to f, and passenger to the new Passenger object.
7. Append the new Reservation to this.reservations.
8. Return true.

       passenger : String - Name of the passenger.

Pre-condition: f is not null. passenger is a non-empty string.
Post-condition: A new Reservation object with a unique ID, status set to PENDING, linked to f and the specified passenger, is appended to this.reservations.}

# Operation Specifications
Airline::addFlight
Summary: Adds a newly created flight to the airline's flight inventory.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: flights
Related Classes: Flight
Input: f : Flight - The flight instance to be registered with the airline. Must not be null.
Output: None (void)
Pre-condition: f is a valid, non-null Flight object.
Post-condition: f is contained in this.flights.

Airline::searchFlights
Summary: Retrieves flights matching specific origin, destination, and departure date criteria.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: flights, Flight.departureAirport, Flight.arrivalAirport, Flight.departureTime, Airport.servesForCities
Related Classes: Flight, Airport, City, EDate, List<Flight>
Input: origin : String - Departure location identifier or city name. dest : String - Arrival location identifier or city name. date : EDate - Target departure date.
Output: List<Flight> - Collection of matching flights. Empty list if none found.
Pre-condition: Input strings and date are valid.
Post-condition: Returns a filtered subset of this.flights satisfying the criteria.

Airline::closeFlight
Summary: Closes an open flight that has not yet departed, deactivates booking, and cancels all confirmed reservations.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: flights, Flight.openForBooking, Flight.reservations, Reservation.status
Related Classes: Flight, Reservation, ReservationStatus, EDate
Input: flightId : String - Unique identifier of the flight to close. now : EDate - Current system time.
Output: boolean - true if flight successfully closed; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: flightId corresponds to an active flight managed by this airline. now represents a valid timestamp.
Post-condition: If successful, flight.openForBooking becomes false and all confirmed reservations are canceled. Returns appropriate boolean.

Airline::publishFlight
Summary: Publishes a new flight for customer booking after validating timestamps, route consistency, and initial status.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: Flight.departureTime, Flight.arrivalTime, Flight.departureAirport, Flight.arrivalAirport, Flight.openForBooking
Related Classes: Flight, Airport, EDate
Input: f : Flight - The flight to be published. now : EDate - Current system time for temporal checks.
Output: boolean - true if publication succeeds; false if validation fails.
Pre-condition: f is not null. Timestamps are parseable.
Post-condition: On success, f.openForBooking is set to true. System state remains unchanged on failure.

Airline::removeFlight
Summary: Removes a flight from the airline's inventory.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: flights
Related Classes: Flight
Input: f : Flight - The flight instance to be removed.
Output: None (void)
Pre-condition: f exists in this.flights.
Post-condition: f is removed from this.flights.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

```text
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

```text
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Operation Specifications
Booking::createReservation
1. Summary: Creates a pending reservation for a specific passenger on a flight within this booking context.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Generate a unique reservation ID (e.g., UUID/GUID).
   - Instantiate new Reservation with id, passenger name converted to Passenger object, f, and status = PENDING.
   - Add reservation to this.reservations.
   - Return true.
3. Related Features: Booking.reservations, Reservation.id, Reservation.status
4. Related Classes: Reservation, Flight, Passenger
5. Input: f : Flight - The target flight for the reservation.
          passenger : String - Name of the passenger.
          now : EDate - Timestamp for audit/logging purposes.
6. Output: boolean - true if reservation created successfully.
7. Pre-condition: f is open for booking. passenger string is valid. Called within a valid booking flow.
8. Post-condition: A new Reservation with status PENDING is added to this.reservations. Returns true.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Operation Specifications
Airline::addFlight
1. Summary: Adds a newly created flight to the airline's managed flight inventory.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Verify f is not null.
   - Check if f.id already exists in this.flights to prevent duplication.
   - If valid, append f to this.flights.
3. Related Features: Airline.flights
4. Related Classes: Flight
5. Input: f : Flight - The flight object to be added. Must not be null.
6. Output: None (void)
7. Pre-condition: f is not null; f.id does not already exist in this.flights.
8. Post-condition: f is successfully added to this.flights. System state reflects the new inventory.

Airline::searchFlights
1. Summary: Retrieves a list of flights matching specified origin, destination, and departure date.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Initialize empty result list.
   - For each flight in this.flights:
     - Check if origin matches any city served by flight.departureAirport or flight.departureAirport.id.
     - Check if dest matches any city served by flight.arrivalAirport or flight.arrivalAirport.id.
     - Check if flight.departureTime.date() equals date.date().
     - If all match, add to result list.
   - Return result list.
3. Related Features: Airline.flights, Flight.departureAirport, Flight.arrivalAirport, Flight.departureTime, Airport.servesForCities
4. Related Classes: Flight, Airport, City, EDate
5. Input: origin : String - Name or ID of departure city/airport.
          dest : String - Name or ID of arrival city/airport.
          date : EDate - Target departure date.
6. Output: List<Flight> - Matching flights. Empty list if none found.
7. Pre-condition: Strings are non-empty. Date is valid.
8. Post-condition: Returns filtered list. Original airline state remains unchanged.

Airline::closeFlight
1. Summary: Closes an open flight that has not yet departed, marking it as unavailable and canceling all confirmed reservations.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Locate flight using flightId from this.flights.
   - If not found, return false.
   - Check if flight.openForBooking is true. If false, return false.
   - Extract date parts: depDate = flight.departureTime.date(), today = now.date().
   - If depDate == today, return false (closing forbidden on departure day).
   - If now >= flight.departureTime, return false (flight already departed).
   - Set flight.openForBooking = false.
   - For each reservation in flight.reservations: if reservation.status == CONFIRMED, set reservation.status = CANCELED.
   - Return true.
3. Related Features: Airline.flights, Flight.openForBooking, Flight.departureTime, Flight.reservations, Reservation.status
4. Related Classes: Flight, Reservation, EDate
5. Input: flightId : String - Unique identifier of the flight to close.
          now : EDate - Current system timestamp for temporal validations.
6. Output: boolean - true if flight was successfully closed and reservations canceled; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: Flight with flightId exists and is currently open for booking. Current time is strictly before departure time. Departure date differs from current date.
8. Post-condition: flight.openForBooking is false. All confirmed reservations on this flight are changed to CANCELED. Returns true.

Airline::publishFlight
1. Summary: Publishes a flight for customer booking after validating temporal consistency, route integrity, and publishing rules.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Validate f.arrivalTime and f.departureTime format (yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss). Parse to EDate.
   - Check temporal consistency: now < f.departureTime < f.arrivalTime. If invalid, return false.
   - Check route integrity: f.departureAirport.id ≠ f.arrivalAirport.id. If equal, return false.
   - Check publishing rule: f.openForBooking must be false (not previously published). If true, return false.
   - Set f.openForBooking = true.
   - Return true.
3. Related Features: Flight.arrivalTime, Flight.departureTime, Flight.departureAirport, Flight.arrivalAirport, Flight.openForBooking
4. Related Classes: Flight, Airport, EDate
5. Input: f : Flight - The flight object to publish.
          now : EDate - Current system timestamp for consistency checks.
6. Output: boolean - true if flight is successfully published; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: f is valid and not previously published (openForBooking == false). Timestamps are well-formed and logically ordered. Departure and arrival airports differ.
8. Post-condition: f.openForBooking is set to true, making it available for bookings. Returns true.

Airline::removeFlight
1. Summary: Removes a flight from the airline's inventory after ensuring it has no active bookings.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Verify f exists in this.flights.
   - Check if f.reservations contains any PENDING or CONFIRMED reservations. If yes, abort (data integrity protection).
   - Remove f from this.flights.
3. Related Features: Airline.flights, Flight.reservations
4. Related Classes: Flight, Reservation, ReservationStatus
5. Input: f : Flight - The flight object to remove.
6. Output: None (void)
7. Pre-condition: f is present in this.flights; f.reservations is empty or contains only CANCELED reservations.
8. Post-condition: f is removed from this.flights. No operational data loss occurs.

5. Input: f : Flight - The flight object to publish.
          now : EDate - Current system timestamp for consistency checks.
6. Output: boolean - true if flight is successfully published; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: f is valid and not previously published (openForBooking == false). Timestamps are well-formed and logically ordered. Departure and arrival airports differ.
8. Post-condition: f.openForBooking is set to true, making it available for bookings. Returns true.

Airline::removeFlight
1. Summary: Removes a flight from the airline's inventory after ensuring it has no active bookings.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Verify f exists in this.flights.
   - Check if f.reservations contains any PENDING or CONFIRMED reservations. If yes, abort (data integrity protection).
   - Remove f from this.flights.
3. Related Features: Airline.flights, Flight.reservations
4. Related Classes: Flight, Reservation, ReservationStatus
5. Input: f : Flight - The flight object to remove.
6. Output: None (void)
7. Pre-condition: f is present in this.flights; f.reservations is empty or contains only CANCELED reservations.
8. Post-condition: f is removed from this.flights. No operational data loss occurs.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Operation Specifications
Flight::addStopover
1. Summary: Inserts a stopover into the flight's travel itinerary.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Validate stop is not null.
   - Check temporal feasibility: flight.departureTime < stop.departureTime < stop.arrivalTime < flight.arrivalTime.
   - Check for overlapping stopovers in stopovers list.
   - If valid, append stop to stopovers.
   - Return true. Else return false.
3. Related Features: Flight.stopovers, Flight.departureTime, Flight.arrivalTime
4. Related Classes: Stopover, EDate
5. Input: stop : Stopover - The stopover details including airport and times.
          now : EDate - Audit timestamp.
6. Output: boolean - true if stopover added; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: stop fits within flight's total schedule without time conflicts. stopover list allows insertion.
8. Post-condition: stop is appended to this.stopovers in chronological order. Returns true.

Flight::removeStopover
1. Summary: Removes a previously added stopover from the flight's itinerary.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Verify stop exists in this.stopovers.
   - If not found, return false.
   - Remove stop from this.stopovers.
   - Return true.
3. Related Features: Flight.stopovers
4. Related Classes: Stopover
5. Input: stop : Stopover - The stopover object to remove.
          now : EDate - Audit timestamp.
6. Output: boolean - true if removal successful; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: stop is currently part of this.stopovers.
8. Post-condition: stop is removed from this.stopovers. Returns true.

Flight::getConfirmedReservations
1. Summary: Retrieves all confirmed reservations for this flight, subject to booking availability.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Check if this.openForBooking is true. If false, return empty list.
   - Initialize empty list confirmedList.
   - For each reservation in this.reservations: if reservation.status == CONFIRMED, add to confirmedList.
   - Return confirmedList.
3. Related Features: Flight.reservations, Flight.openForBooking, Reservation.status
4. Related Classes: Reservation, ReservationStatus
5. Input: None (operates on instance state)
6. Output: List<Reservation> - Collection of confirmed reservations, or empty list if none/openForBooking is false.
7. Pre-condition: this.openForBooking is true.
8. Post-condition: Returns filtered list of confirmed reservations. System state remains unchanged.

```result
 * revised description for operation_1
 */

class_1::operation_2
/*
 * revised description for this operation_2
 */
```

6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title flights Model

class Airline {
    flights : List<Flight>
    addFlight(f : Flight) : void
    closeFlight(flightId : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    publishFlight(f : Flight, now : EDate) : boolean
    removeFlight(f : Flight) : void
    searchFlights(origin : String, dest : String, date : EDate) : List<Flight>
}
class Airport {
    id : String
    servesForCities : List<City>
    addCity(c : City) : void
}
class Booking {
    customer : Customer
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    createReservation(f : Flight, passenger : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class City {
}
class Customer {
    bookings : List<Booking>
    addBooking(f : Flight, now : EDate, listOfPassengerNames : List<String>) : boolean
    cancel(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    confirm(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Flight {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    id : String
    openForBooking : boolean
    arrivalAirport : Airport
    departureAirport : Airport
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    stopovers : List<Stopover>
    addStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
    getConfirmedReservations() : List<Reservation>
    removeStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Passenger {
    name : String
}
class Reservation {
    id : String
    status : ReservationStatus
    flight : Flight
    passenger : Passenger
}
class Stopover {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    airport : Airport
}
enum ReservationStatus {
    PENDING
    CONFIRMED
    CANCELED
}

Airline *-- "*" Flight : flights
Airport --> "*" City : servesForCities
Booking --> Customer : customer
Booking *-- "*" Reservation : reservations
Customer --> "*" Booking : bookings
Flight --> Airport : arrivalAirport
Flight --> Airport : departureAirport
Flight --> "*" Reservation : reservations
Flight *-- "*" Stopover : stopovers
Reservation --> Flight : flight
Reservation --> Passenger : passenger
Stopover --> Airport : airport
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Flight::addStopover
1. Summary: Inserts a stopover into the flight's travel itinerary.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Validate stop is not null.
   - Check temporal feasibility: flight.departureTime < stop.departureTime < stop.arrivalTime < flight.arrivalTime.
   - Check for overlapping stopovers in stopovers list.
   - If valid, append stop to stopovers.
   - Return true. Else return false.
3. Related Features: Flight.stopovers, Flight.departureTime, Flight.arrivalTime
4. Related Classes: Stopover, EDate
5. Input: stop : Stopover - The stopover details including airport and times.
          now : EDate - Audit timestamp.
6. Output: boolean - true if stopover added; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: stop fits within flight's total schedule without time conflicts. stopover list allows insertion.
8. Post-condition: stop is appended to this.stopovers in chronological order. Returns true.

Flight::removeStopover
1. Summary: Removes a previously added stopover from the flight's itinerary.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Verify stop exists in this.stopovers.
   - If not found, return false.
   - Remove stop from this.stopovers.
   - Return true.
3. Related Features: Flight.stopovers
4. Related Classes: Stopover
5. Input: stop : Stopover - The stopover object to remove.
          now : EDate - Audit timestamp.
6. Output: boolean - true if removal successful; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: stop is currently part of this.stopovers.
8. Post-condition: stop is removed from this.stopovers. Returns true.

Flight::getConfirmedReservations
1. Summary: Retrieves all confirmed reservations for this flight, subject to booking availability.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Check if this.openForBooking is true. If false, return empty list.
   - Initialize empty list confirmedList.
   - For each reservation in this.reservations: if reservation.status == CONFIRMED, add to confirmedList.
   - Return confirmedList.
3. Related Features: Flight.reservations, Flight.openForBooking, Reservation.status
4. Related Classes: Reservation, ReservationStatus
5. Input: None (operates on instance state)
6. Output: List<Reservation> - Collection of confirmed reservations, or empty list if none/openForBooking is false.
7. Pre-condition: this.openForBooking is true.
8. Post-condition: Returns filtered list of confirmed reservations. System state remains unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
Customer::addBooking
1. Summary: Initiates a new booking for a customer on a flight with multiple passengers, performing global and local validations.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Validate listOfPassengerNames contains no duplicates. If duplicates found, return false.
   - Check if f.openForBooking is true. If false, return false.
   - Check if now < f.departureTime. If false, return false.
   - Query existing reservations across all customer.bookings (and optionally global flight pool) to ensure no passenger already has a reservation on f. If conflict, return false.
   - Instantiate new Booking object linked to this customer.
   - For each name in listOfPassengerNames: call booking.createReservation(f, name, now).
   - Add booking to this.bookings.
   - Return true.
3. Related Features: Customer.bookings, Booking.customer, Booking.reservations, Flight.openForBooking, Flight.departureTime
4. Related Classes: Booking, Flight, Passenger, Reservation, EDate
5. Input: f : Flight - Flight to book.
          now : EDate - Current timestamp.
          listOfPassengerNames : List<String> - Names of passengers traveling together.
6. Output: boolean - true if booking and all reservations created successfully; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: f is open for booking. Current time precedes departure. No duplicate names in list. No passenger already booked on f.
8. Post-condition: New Booking containing multiple PENDING Reservations is created and linked to customer. Returns true.

Customer::cancel
1. Summary: Cancels an existing reservation belonging to the customer.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Traverse this.bookings to locate reservation with id == reservationID.
   - If not found, return false.
   - Get associated flight f.
   - Check if now < f.departureTime. If false, return false.
   - Check if f.openForBooking is true. If false, return false.
   - If reservation.status == CANCELED, return false (already canceled).
   - Set reservation.status = CANCELED.
   - Return true.
3. Related Features: Customer.bookings, Booking.reservations, Reservation.status, Flight.openForBooking, Flight.departureTime
4. Related Classes: Booking, Reservation, Flight, EDate
5. Input: reservationID : String - Unique ID of the reservation to cancel.
          now : EDate - Current timestamp for validation.
6. Output: boolean - true if cancellation successful; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: Reservation exists and belongs to this customer. Flight has not departed and is still open for booking. Status is not already CANCELED.
8. Post-condition: Reservation status is updated to CANCELED. Returns true.

Customer::confirm
1. Summary: Confirms an existing pending reservation belonging to the customer.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Traverse this.bookings to locate reservation with id == reservationID.
   - If not found, return false.
   - Get associated flight f.
   - Check if now < f.departureTime. If false, return false.
   - Check if f.openForBooking is true. If false, return false.
   - If reservation.status != PENDING, return false.
   - Set reservation.status = CONFIRMED.
   - Return true.
3. Related Features: Customer.bookings, Booking.reservations, Reservation.status, Flight.openForBooking, Flight.departureTime
4. Related Classes: Booking, Reservation, Flight, EDate
5. Input: reservationID : String - Unique ID of the reservation to confirm.
          now : EDate - Current timestamp for validation.
6. Output: boolean - true if confirmation successful; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: Reservation exists and belongs to this customer. Flight has not departed and is still open for booking. Status is PENDING.
8. Post-condition: Reservation status is updated to CONFIRMED. Returns true.

1. Summary: Confirms an existing pending reservation belonging to the customer.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Traverse this.bookings to locate reservation with id == reservationID.
   - If not found, return false.
   - Get associated flight f.
   - Check if now < f.departureTime. If false, return false.
   - Check if f.openForBooking is true. If false, return false.
   - If reservation.status != PENDING, return false.
   - Set reservation.status = CONFIRMED.
   - Return true.
3. Related Features: Customer.bookings, Booking.reservations, Reservation.status, Flight.openForBooking, Flight.departureTime
4. Related Classes: Booking, Reservation, Flight, EDate
5. Input: reservationID : String - Unique ID of the reservation to confirm.
          now : EDate - Current timestamp for validation.
6. Output: boolean - true if confirmation successful; false otherwise.
7. Pre-condition: Reservation exists and belongs to this customer. Flight has not departed and is still open for booking. Status is PENDING.
8. Post-condition: Reservation status is updated to CONFIRMED. Returns true.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean addStopover(final Stopover stop, final Date now)
  {
    if (stop == null) {
      return false;
    }
    if (this.departureTime == null || this.arrivalTime == null ||
        stop.getDepartureTime() == null || stop.getArrivalTime() == null) {
      return false;
    }
    // Check temporal feasibility: flight.departureTime < stop.departureTime < stop.arrivalTime < flight.arrivalTime
    if (this.departureTime.compareTo(stop.getDepartureTime()) >= 0 ||
        stop.getDepartureTime().compareTo(stop.getArrivalTime()) >= 0 ||
        stop.getArrivalTime().compareTo(this.arrivalTime) >= 0) {
      return false;
    }
    // Check for overlapping stopovers
    for (Stopover existingStop : getStopovers()) {
      if (existingStop.getDepartureTime() != null && existingStop.getArrivalTime() != null) {
        // Two intervals overlap if neither ends before the other starts
        if (!(stop.getArrivalTime().compareTo(existingStop.getDepartureTime()) <= 0 ||
              stop.getDepartureTime().compareTo(existingStop.getArrivalTime()) >= 0)) {
          return false;
        }
      }
    }
    getStopovers().add(stop);
    return true;
  }

      return false;
    }

    // Validate passenger uniqueness: iterate through f.reservations; if any existing reservation's passenger name matches the input passenger string, return false.
    for (Reservation existingRes : f.getReservations()) {
      if (passenger != null && existingRes.getPassenger() != null && passenger.equals(existingRes.getPassenger().getName())) {
        return false;
      }

    String id = UUID.randomUUID().toString();

    // Instantiate a new Passenger object with the given passenger name.
    Passenger newPassenger = FlightsFactory.eINSTANCE.createPassenger();
    newPassenger.setName(passenger);

    this.getReservations().add(newReservation);

    // Return true.
    return true;

```java
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - Validate listOfPassengerNames contains no internal duplicates. If duplicates found, return false.
   *    - Check if f.openForBooking is true. If false, return false.
   *    - Check if now < f.departureTime. If false, return false.
```

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  public boolean addBooking(final Flight f, final Date now, final EList<String> listOfPassengerNames)
  {
    // Validate listOfPassengerNames contains no internal duplicates
    Set<String> seenNames = new HashSet<>(listOfPassengerNames);
    if (seenNames.size() != listOfPassengerNames.size()) {
      return false;
    }

    // Check if f.openForBooking is true
    if (!f.isOpenForBooking()) {
      return false;
    }

    // Check if now < f.departureTime
    if (!now.before(f.getDepartureTime())) {
      return false;
    }

    // Query f.reservations to ensure no existing reservation has a matching passenger name
    for (Reservation existingRes : f.getReservations()) {
      if (listOfPassengerNames.contains(existingRes.getPassengerName())) {
        return false;
      }
    }

    // Instantiate new Booking object, set customer to this, and add to this.bookings
    Booking booking = FlightsFactory.eINSTANCE.createBooking();
    booking.setCustomer(this);
    this.getBookings().add(booking);

    // For each name in listOfPassengerNames: call booking.createReservation(f, name, now)
    int reservationIndex = 0;
    for (String name : listOfPassengerNames) {
      Reservation reservation = booking.createReservation(f, name, now);
      // Assign a unique ID
      reservation.setReservationID(UUID.randomUUID().toString());
      // Initialize with status PENDING
      reservation.setStatus(ReservationStatus.PENDING);
    }

    return true;
  }

        if (reservationID.equals(r.getReservationID())) {
          // Retrieve the associated flight f
          Flight f = r.getFlight();
          
          // Check if now < f.departureTime

            return false;
          }
          
          // Check if f.openForBooking is true

            return false;

            return false;
          }

import org.eclipse.emf.ecore.util.EObjectContainmentEList;
import org.eclipse.emf.ecore.util.InternalEList;

/**
 * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
 * A representation of the model object '<em><b>Airline</b></em>'.
 * <!-- end-user-doc -->
 *
 * <p>
 * The following features are supported:
 * </p>
 * <ul>
 *   <li>{@link edu.flights.Airline#getFlights <em>Flights</em>}</li>
 * </ul>
 *
 * @see edu.flights.FlightsPackage#getAirline()
 * @model kind="class"
 * @generated NOT
 */
public class Airline extends MinimalEObjectImpl.Container implements EObject
{
  protected EList<Flight> flights;

  protected Airline()
  { ... /* omitted */ }

  @Override
  protected EClass eStaticClass()
  { ... /* omitted */ }

  public EList<Flight> getFlights()
  { ... /* omitted */ }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * 1. Summary: Adds a newly created flight to the airline's managed flight inventory.
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - Verify f is not null.
   *    - Check if f.id already exists in this.flights to prevent duplication.
   *    - If valid, append f to this.flights.
   * 3. Related Features: Airline.flights
   * 4. Related Classes: Flight
   * 5. Input: f : Flight - The flight object to be added. Must not be null.
   * 6. Output: None (void)
   * 7. Pre-condition: f is not null; f.id does not already exist in this.flights.
   * 8. Post-condition: f is successfully added to this.flights. System state reflects the new inventory.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void addFlight(final Flight f)
  {
    if (f == null) {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("Flight cannot be null");
    }
    for (Flight existing : getFlights()) {
        if (existing.getId().equals(f.getId())) {
            return;
        }
    }
    getFlights().add(f);
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * 1. Summary: Removes a flight from the airline's inventory after ensuring it has no active bookings.
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - Verify f exists in this.flights.
   *    - Check if f.reservations contains any PENDING or CONFIRMED reservations. If yes, abort (data integrity protection).
   *    - Remove f from this.flights.
   * 3. Related Features: Airline.flights, Flight.reservations
   * 4. Related Classes: Flight, Reservation, ReservationStatus
   * 5. Input: f : Flight - The flight object to remove.
   * 6. Output: None (void)
   * 7. Pre-condition: f is present in this.flights; f.reservations is empty or contains only CANCELED reservations.
   * 8. Post-condition: f is removed from this.flights. No operational data loss occurs.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void removeFlight(final Flight f)
  {
    if (!getFlights().contains(f)) {
        return;
    }
    for (Reservation r : f.getReservations()) {
        if (r.getStatus() == ReservationStatus.PENDING || r.getStatus() == ReservationStatus.CONFIRMED) {
            return;
        }
    }
    getFlights().remove(f);
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * 1. Summary: Publishes a flight for customer booking after validating temporal consistency, route integrity, and publishing rules.
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - Validate f.arrivalTime and f.departureTime format (yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss). Parse to EDate. If invalid, return false.
   *    - Check temporal consistency: now < f.departureTime < f.arrivalTime. If invalid, return false.
   *    - Check route integrity: f.departureAirport.id ≠ f.arrivalAirport.id. If equal, return false.
   *    - Check publishing rule: f.openForBooking must be false (not previously published). If true, return false.
   *    - Set f.openForBooking = true.
   *    - Return true.
   * 3. Related Features: Flight.arrivalTime, Flight.departureTime, Flight.departureAirport, Flight.arrivalAirport, Flight.openForBooking
   * 4. Related Classes: Flight, Airport, EDate
   * 5. Input: f : Flight - The flight object to publish.
   *          now : EDate - Current system timestamp for consistency checks.
   * 6. Output: boolean - true if flight is successfully published; false otherwise.
   * 7. Pre-condition: f is not null and represents a valid flight ready for publication.
   * 8. Post-condition: f.openForBooking is set to true, making it available for bookings. Method returns true.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean publishFlight(final Flight f, final Date now)
  {
    try {
        Date depTime = f.getDepartureTime();
        Date arrTime = f.getArrivalTime();
        
        if (now.before(depTime) && depTime.before(arrTime) &&
            !f.getDepartureAirport().getId().equals(f.getArrivalAirport().getId()) &&
            !f.isOpenForBooking()) {
            f.setOpenForBooking(true);
            return true;
        }
    } catch (Exception e) {
        return false;
    }
    return false;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * 1. Summary: Closes an open flight that has not yet departed, marking it as unavailable and canceling all confirmed reservations.
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - Locate flight using flightId from this.flights. If not found, return false.
   *    - Check if flight.openForBooking is true. If false, return false.
   *    - Extract date parts: depDate = flight.departureTime.date(), today = now.date().
   *    - If depDate == today, return false (closing forbidden on departure day).
   *    - If now >= flight.departureTime, return false (flight already departed).
   *    - Set flight.openForBooking = false.
   *    - For each reservation in flight.reservations: if reservation.status == CONFIRMED, set reservation.status = CANCELED.
   *    - Return true.
   * 3. Related Features: Airline.flights, Flight.openForBooking, Flight.departureTime, Flight.reservations, Reservation.status
   * 4. Related Classes: Flight, Reservation, EDate
   * 5. Input: flightId : String - Unique identifier of the flight to close.
   *          now : EDate - Current system timestamp for temporal validations.
   * 6. Output: boolean - true if flight was successfully closed and reservations canceled; false otherwise.
   * 7. Pre-condition: Flight with flightId exists in this.flights.
   * 8. Post-condition: flight.openForBooking is false. All confirmed reservations on this flight are updated to CANCELED. Method returns true.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean closeFlight(final String flightId, final Date now)
  {
    Flight target = null;
    for (Flight fl : getFlights()) {
        if (fl.getId().equals(flightId)) {
            target = fl;
            break;
        }
    }
    if (target == null) return false;
    if (!target.isOpenForBooking()) return false;

    Calendar depCal = Calendar.getInstance();
    depCal.setTime(target.getDepartureTime());
    Calendar nowCal = Calendar.getInstance();
    nowCal.setTime(now);

    if (depCal.get(Calendar.YEAR) == nowCal.get(Calendar.YEAR) &&
        depCal.get(Calendar.MONTH) == nowCal.get(Calendar.MONTH) &&
        depCal.get(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH) == nowCal.get(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH)) {
        return false;
    }
    if (now.compareTo(target.getDepartureTime()) >= 0) {
        return false;
    }

    target.setOpenForBooking(false);
    for (Reservation r : target.getReservations()) {
        if (r.getStatus() == ReservationStatus.CONFIRMED) {
            r.setStatus(ReservationStatus.CANCELED);
        }
    }
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * 1. Summary: Retrieves a list of flights matching specified origin, destination, and departure date.
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - Initialize empty result list.
   *    - For each flight in this.flights:
   *      - Check if origin matches any city served by flight.departureAirport or flight.departureAirport.id.
   *      - Check if dest matches any city served by flight.arrivalAirport or flight.arrivalAirport.id.
   *      - Check if flight.departureTime.date() equals date.date().
   *      - If all match, add to result list.
   *    - Return result list.
   * 3. Related Features: Airline.flights, Flight.departureAirport, Flight.arrivalAirport, Flight.departureTime, Airport.servesForCities
   * 4. Related Classes: Flight, Airport, City, EDate
   * 5. Input: origin : String - Name or ID of departure city/airport.
   *           dest : String - Name or ID of arrival city/airport.
   *           date : EDate - Target departure date.
   * 6. Output: List<Flight> - Matching flights. Empty list if none found.
   * 7. Pre-condition: Strings are non-empty. Date is valid.
   * 8. Post-condition: Returns filtered list. Original airline state remains unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Flight> searchFlights(final String origin, final String dest, final Date date)
  {
    EList<Flight> result = new BasicEList<>();
    Calendar searchCal = Calendar.getInstance();
    searchCal.setTime(date);
    
    for (Flight fl : getFlights()) {
        Calendar depCal = Calendar.getInstance();
        depCal.setTime(fl.getDepartureTime());
        
        boolean dateMatch = (depCal.get(Calendar.YEAR) == searchCal.get(Calendar.YEAR) &&
                             depCal.get(Calendar.MONTH) == searchCal.get(Calendar.MONTH) &&
                             depCal.get(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH) == searchCal.get(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH));
                             
        boolean originMatch = fl.getDepartureAirport().getId().equals(origin);
        if (!originMatch) {
            EList<String> cities = fl.getDepartureAirport().getServesForCities();
            if (cities != null && cities.contains(origin)) {
                originMatch = true;
            }
        }
                        
        boolean destMatch = fl.getArrivalAirport().getId().equals(dest);
        if (!destMatch) {
            EList<String> cities = fl.getArrivalAirport().getServesForCities();
            if (cities != null && cities.contains(dest)) {
                destMatch = true;
            }
        }
                        
        if (dateMatch && originMatch && destMatch) {
            result.add(fl);
        }
    }
    return result;
  }

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

```java
   * 2. Algorithm:

   *    - For each flight in this.flights:
   *      - Check if origin matches any city served by flight.departureAirport or flight.departureAirport.id.
   *      - Check if dest matches any city served by flight.arrivalAirport or flight.arrivalAirport.id.
   *      - Check if flight.departureTime.date() equals date.date().
   *      - If all match, add to result list.
   *    - Return result list.
   * 3. Related Features: Airline.flights, Flight.departureAirport, Flight.arrivalAirport, Flight.departureTime, Airport.servesForCities
   * 4. Related Classes: Flight, Airport, City, EDate
   * 5. Input: origin : String - Name or ID of departure city/airport.
   *           dest : String - Name or ID of arrival city/airport.
   *           date : EDate - Target departure date.
   * 6. Output: List<Flight> - Matching flights. Empty list if none found.
   * 7. Pre-condition: Strings are non-empty. Date is valid.
   * 8. Post-condition: Returns filtered list. Original airline state remains unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Flight> searchFlights(final String origin, final String dest, final Date date)
  {
    EList<Flight> result = new BasicEList<>();
    Calendar searchCal = Calendar.getInstance();
    searchCal.setTime(date);
    
    for (Flight fl : getFlights()) {
        Calendar depCal = Calendar.getInstance();
        depCal.setTime(fl.getDepartureTime());
        
        boolean dateMatch = (depCal.get(Calendar.YEAR) == searchCal.get(Calendar.YEAR) &&
                             depCal.get(Calendar.MONTH) == searchCal.get(Calendar.MONTH) &&
                             depCal.get(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH) == searchCal.get(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH));
                             
        boolean originMatch = fl.getDepartureAirport().getId().equals(origin);
        if (!originMatch) {
            EList<String> cities = fl.getDepartureAirport().getServesForCities();
            if (cities != null && cities.contains(origin)) {
                originMatch = true;
            }
        }
                        
        boolean destMatch = fl.getArrivalAirport().getId().equals(dest);
        if (!destMatch) {
            EList<String> cities = fl.getArrivalAirport().getServesForCities();
            if (cities != null && cities.contains(dest)) {
                destMatch = true;
            }
        }
                        
        if (dateMatch && originMatch && destMatch) {
            result.add(fl);
        }
    }
    return result;
  }
```

```java
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
```
```java
   * 1. Summary: Creates a pending reservation for a specific passenger on a flight within this booking context. Validates flight timing and passenger uniqueness before creation.
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - Validate temporal constraint: if now >= f.departureTime, return false.
   *    - Validate passenger uniqueness: iterate through f.reservations; if any existing reservation's passenger name matches the input passenger string, return false.
   *    - Generate a unique reservation ID (e.g., UUID/GUID).
   *    - Instantiate a new Passenger object with the given passenger name.
   *    - Instantiate a new Reservation with the generated ID, the new Passenger object, f as the flight reference, and status set to PENDING.
   *    - Add the new Reservation to this.reservations.
   *    - Return true.
   * 3. Related Features: Booking.reservations
   * 4. Related Classes: Reservation, Flight, Passenger
   * 5. Input: f : Flight - The target flight for the reservation.
   *          passenger : String - Name of the passenger.
   *          now : EDate - Current timestamp used for validation checks.
   * 6. Output: boolean - true if the reservation is successfully created and added to the booking; false if validation fails (flight already departed or passenger already booked on this flight).
   * 7. Pre-condition: f is a valid Flight object. now represents the current system time. passenger is a valid, non-null string.
   * 8. Post-condition: On success, a new Reservation with status PENDING is appended to this.reservations. On failure, the system state remains unchanged and false is returned.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean createReservation(final Flight f, final String passenger, final Date now)
  {
    // Validate temporal constraint: if now >= f.departureTime, return false.
    if (now.compareTo(f.getDepartureTime()) >= 0) {
      return false;
    }

    // Validate passenger uniqueness: iterate through f.reservations; if any existing reservation's passenger name matches the input passenger string, return false.
    for (Reservation existingRes : f.getReservations()) {
      if (passenger != null && existingRes.getPassenger() != null && passenger.equals(existingRes.getPassenger().getName())) {
        return false;
      }
    }

    // Generate a unique reservation ID (e.g., UUID/GUID).
    String id = UUID.randomUUID().toString();

    // Instantiate a new Passenger object with the given passenger name.
    Passenger newPassenger = FlightsFactory.eINSTANCE.createPassenger();
    newPassenger.setName(passenger);

    // Instantiate a new Reservation with the generated ID, the new Passenger object, f as the flight reference, and status set to PENDING.
    Reservation newReservation = FlightsFactory.eINSTANCE.createReservation();
    newReservation.setId(id);
    newReservation.setPassenger(newPassenger);
    newReservation.setFlight(f);
    newReservation.setStatus(FlightsPackage.eINSTANCE.getReservationStatus().getLiterals().get("PENDING"));

    // Add the new Reservation to this.reservations.
    this.getReservations().add(newReservation);

    // Return true.
    return true;
  }
```

```java
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean createReservation(final Flight f, final String passenger, final Date now)
  {
    // Validate temporal constraint: if now >= f.departureTime, return false.
    if (now.compareTo(f.getDepartureTime()) >= 0) {
      return false;
    }

    // Validate passenger uniqueness: iterate through f.reservations; if any existing reservation's passenger name matches the input passenger string, return false.
    for (Reservation existingRes : f.getReservations()) {
      if (passenger != null && existingRes.getPassenger() != null && passenger.equals(existingRes.getPassenger().getName())) {
        return false;
      }
    }

    // Generate a unique reservation ID (e.g., UUID/GUID).
    String id = UUID.randomUUID().toString();

    // Instantiate a new Passenger object with the given passenger name.
    Passenger newPassenger = FlightsFactory.eINSTANCE.createPassenger();
    newPassenger.setName(passenger);

    newReservation.setPassenger(newPassenger);
    newReservation.setFlight(f);
    newReservation.setStatus(FlightsPackage.eINSTANCE.getReservationStatus().getLiterals().get("PENDING"));

    // Add the new Reservation to this.reservations.
    this.getReservations().add(newReservation);
```

   *    - Return true.

```java
   * 4. Related Classes: Booking, Flight, Reservation, Passenger, List, EDate
   * 5. Input: f : Flight - Flight to book.
   *          now : EDate - Current timestamp.
   *          listOfPassengerNames : List<String> - Names of passengers traveling together.
   * 6. Output: boolean - true if booking and all reservations created successfully; false otherwise.
   * 7. Pre-condition: f is open for booking. Current time precedes departure. No duplicate names in list. No passenger in the list already has a reservation on f.
   * 8. Post-condition: New Booking is created and linked to customer. Multiple Reservations are generated with unique IDs and PENDING status, linked to the booking and flight. Returns true.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model listOfPassengerNamesMany="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean addBooking(final Flight f, final Date now, final EList<String> listOfPassengerNames)
  {
    // Validate listOfPassengerNames contains no internal duplicates
    Set<String> seenNames = new HashSet<>(listOfPassengerNames);
    if (seenNames.size() != listOfPassengerNames.size()) {
      return false;
    }

    // Check if f.openForBooking is true
    if (!f.isOpenForBooking()) {
      return false;
    }

    // Check if now < f.departureTime
    if (!now.before(f.getDepartureTime())) {
      return false;
    }

    // Query f.reservations to ensure no existing reservation has a matching passenger name
    for (Reservation existingRes : f.getReservations()) {
      if (listOfPassengerNames.contains(existingRes.getPassengerName())) {
        return false;
      }
    }

    // Instantiate new Booking object, set customer to this, and add to this.bookings
    Booking booking = FlightsFactory.eINSTANCE.createBooking();
    booking.setCustomer(this);
    this.getBookings().add(booking);

    // For each name in listOfPassengerNames: call booking.createReservation(f, name, now)
    int reservationIndex = 0;
    for (String name : listOfPassengerNames) {
      Reservation reservation = booking.createReservation(f, name, now);
      // Assign a unique ID
      reservation.setReservationID(UUID.randomUUID().toString());
      // Initialize with status PENDING
      reservation.setStatus(ReservationStatus.PENDING);
    }

    return true;
  }
```
